## 1. The words used here, explained before they are used

- **The analysis** — the harmonic-analysis software this project builds: given a notated score it decides the
  tonality, the chords, and the moments at which one chord gives way to the next, and from those it says what else a
  harmonic analysis of the music says.
- **The derivation** — `cowork_blind_derivation_l3_2026_10_17.md`: the statements and open questions written by a
  session that read a brief, a ten-member boot pack, the two input-contract files, three staged scores with their
  published analyses, and nothing else of this project. Its statements are cited **L3-S1** to **L3-S42**, its open
  questions **OQ-L3-1** to **OQ-L3-14**. Each statement carries six fields: the statement, its defense, its source
  class, its status, its premise with that premise's false-negative path, and what would falsify it.
- **L3** — in the ratified charter's own words (`FRAMEWORK.md` §5), *"The read-off facts"*. §2 re-explains it.
- **The input contracts** — the two ratified specifications the deriving session was given as fixed: the L0/L1
  specification, whose statements the derivation cites as **IC-L1 S-n**, and the L2 specification, whose statements
  it cites as **L2-Sn**. This file uses the same citations.
- **The outgoing text** — the current texts describing what L3's charter now covers. Under the ruled phase definition
  the outgoing text is an **untrusted source**: evidence about the present text, read after the derivation, and
  **never authority over a derived statement**. A disagreement between the two is recorded here and resolved nowhere.
- **The outgoing population** — the passages of the outgoing text this comparison covers, fixed by the two rulings
  named in the banner and derived by `tools/audit/gen_l3_outgoing_population.py`.
- **A member** — one unit of the tabulation, taken in the order the artifact publishes: one of the six named
  `ARCHITECTURE.md` places; one whole document; the passages of one document (for `ARCHITECTURE.md`, the passages under
  one top-level `## ` heading); or one of the two transfer inputs.
- **A passage** — the block of lines around one term-search hit, or around one decision's home, cut by the passage
  rule published on the artifact (`the_tabulation_population` → `the_passage_rule`). A passage member is made of such
  blocks; its line ranges are published with it.
- **The residue** — the files the term search hit that are outside the ruled specification document set. They are
  published on the artifact, dispositioned by nobody here, and left to the mining map.
- **An outgoing statement** — the unit of the tabulation; §4 fixes it.
- **A disposition** — one of the five fates the phase definition names for an outgoing statement; §5 states them and
  that the set is closed.
- **WITHHELD** and **SEEN** — the two marks a row carries about what the deriving session could have read; §5 fixes
  both, narrowly.
- **The transfer list** — the parking list for content that belongs to another charter, so that nothing is silently
  dropped and nothing is absorbed into the wrong charter (§10).
- **The transfer input** — the rows the L0/L1 and L2 comparisons placed RELOCATED to L3, listed under *To L3* in §10 of
  each of those two comparisons' reading files; they are the last two members here.
- **View, decision, harmonic span, reading, principal reading, rival, mass, phrase end, cadential arrival, elided
  phrase boundary, structural bass** — used in the derivation's own senses (its §0): a *view* is an L3 fact computed
  from facts L2 has already decided, deciding nothing; an *L3 decision* is an L3 fact L2's facts do not fix, which L3
  settles among rivals; a *harmonic span* is one stretch of L2's segmentation carrying one tonality and one chord; a
  *reading* is one complete candidate answer for the stretch of music being read, the *principal reading* the one
  committed; a *rival* is an alternative published beside it; *mass* is the model's own weight of a reading; a *phrase
  end* is the last moment of a phrase as L3 decides it; the *cadential arrival* is where the final harmony of a
  cadential progression begins; an *elided phrase boundary* is one position that both closes one phrase's cadence and
  begins the next phrase; the *structural bass* is the chord member L2's figure names as the bass of a span.

**On reserved words, because this file is read beside a music-analysis specification.** Bare *score* is the music, and
the numerical sense is always *candidate score* or another qualified form; bare *key* is tonality; bare *bar* is the
metric unit and the prohibiting sense is written *exclude*; bare *note* is a pitch event; bare *measure* as a noun is
the bar, and the gauging sense is *measurement*; *figure* is the figure of a chord or a figured-bass figure, and a
quantity is called a *value* or a *count*; *measurement tool* or *script* is written where the collided word would be
wrong. **No numeric grade appears anywhere in this file**: the vocabulary of §5 is the whole of the verdict language.

## 2. The subject, re-explained from scratch

Once a harmonic analysis has decided the music — the tonality at each moment, where each harmony gives way to the
next, which sounding notes belong to the harmony and which decorate it, and what chord is read over each stretch — a
good deal more can be said about it without deciding anything new, and some further things can be said only by
deciding something new. **L3 is the layer that says them.**

The charter's question, in its own words: *given a settled tonal reading, what else does a harmonic analysis of this
music say?* It publishes five things. **The cadence at each phrase end, with its type** — a half cadence, an authentic
cadence, a deceptive close, and so on. **The phrase and section grouping** — where the phrases end, and how phrases
group into larger sections. **The chord symbol** — the chord written as a root, a quality and a bass note, the way a
lead sheet writes it, read off the decided chord. **The figured bass** — the thoroughbass figures, the intervals above
the bass, describing the decided harmony. **The harmonic rhythm** — the pattern in time of the changes of harmony,
read against the meter.

The charter fixes three things about how. The chord symbol is a **view** of the decided chord: its root is the tonic
transposed by the degree's interval, and nothing is settled in computing it. The notated phrase evidence — fermatas,
rests, repeats, double bar lines — is read by the layer beneath, and **which of it marks a phrase end is decided at L3**,
the decisive further evidence being cadential. And **a read-off fact may never revise the decision it was read off**:
nothing L3 publishes reaches back into L2's reading.

**The question this file answers is neither of those.** A session that had not read this project's specifications or
code was asked how L3 should work, and answered in forty-two statements. This file sets that answer beside the
specifications the project already holds, and gives **every statement of those specifications** in the population
exactly one recorded fate. It settles nothing: it tabulates, so that the user can settle it.

## 4. The unit, fixed before the tabulation begins

An **outgoing statement** is one sentence, one bullet or one table row of a member's text that states what the
analysis does, must do, may assume, or must not do —

- **about L3's subject**: the charter's question — *given a settled tonal reading, what else does a harmonic analysis
  of this music say?*; what L3 publishes — the cadence at each phrase end with its type, the phrase and section
  grouping, the chord symbol (root pitch class, quality and bass note), the figured bass and the harmonic rhythm; and
  the faces the brief's §2 and the derivation's §3 name: (a) the chord symbol, (b) where a phrase ends, (c) the cadence
  at each phrase end and its type, (d) the grouping of phrases into sections, (e) the figured bass, (f) the harmonic
  rhythm, (g) uncertainty on every read-off fact, (h) the boundaries of the layer; **or**
- **about another charter's subject met inside the member** — such a statement is placed RELOCATED.

A sentence that is none of those — narrative, provenance, a test count, a commit identifier, a pointer to another
document — is **not** an outgoing statement, and is listed once per member under *not a statement*, with its kind
named, so that completeness can be checked by arithmetic at the member. **A paragraph is never a unit.** Where one
sentence carries two claims it is two statements, quoted with the same sentence and marked (i) and (ii). **For a
passage member, only the lines inside its published ranges are tabulated**; a sentence that starts before a range or
runs past it is quoted whole and tabulated once, at the member holding its first line inside a range.

## 5. The vocabulary, and that it is CLOSED

Two axes, both closed, both fixed before any placement began. **No other verdict word appears in this file, and no
numeric grade appears anywhere in it.**

**The CURRENT-TEXT AXIS — one per named derived statement, per row.** This axis is evidence about the present text and
is **never a verdict on the derivation**:

- **AGREES** — the derived statement and the outgoing statement say the same thing in substance.
- **DIFFERS** — they say different things. The row then states the difference in one sentence, in both texts' own
  words, **and chooses nothing between them.**
- **THE DERIVATION IS SILENT** — no derived statement speaks to this outgoing statement.

**The DISPOSITION AXIS — exactly one per outgoing statement**, out of the five the phase definition names, plus the
honest default:

- **ADOPTED — carried** — a derived statement already carries this content in substance (named by its L3-S-number).
- **ADOPTED — proposed** — no derived statement carries it; it is not implementation-describing, not another charter's,
  not historical, and the worth test does not discard it. **This is a PROPOSAL to the user that the outgoing content be
  added to the derived specification.** The proposal is written in one sentence beside the row. It is never written
  into the derivation.
- **RELOCATED** — its content belongs to another charter. The row names that charter in `FRAMEWORK.md` §5's own words,
  and the statement is appended to the TRANSFER LIST at §10. Where an input-contract file already carries the content,
  the row names its statement (IC-L1 S-n, or L2-Sn).
- **QUARANTINED** — it states how the implementation currently works, or a measured property of it. The row writes the
  audit question in one sentence.
- **DISCARDED** — under principle #10's worth test, with the finding, the date the row is written and the reason — all
  three, or the disposition does not reach the row.
- **HISTORICAL** — it records an event, a build state, a superseded plan, or a status; not a rule.
- **UNPLACED** — this session cannot defend one disposition in one sentence at the two texts. The row then says what
  was read. **This is the honest default and it is NOT a sixth disposition** — it is a row the user places.

The charters, named in `FRAMEWORK.md` §5's own words wherever a row relocates to one: ***L0 — The notated record***
(the input contract); ***L1 — Change points, candidates and notated evidence***; ***L2 — The tonal reading. The one
entangled decision.***; ***the second axis — voice leading***; and the NOT A LAYER items ***the uncertainty surface***
and ***the measurement of the analysis***.

**THE TWO MARKS, narrow by the dispatch's own choice.** Little of the outgoing text could have been met by the deriving
session: five documents were withheld from its pack by name, and the pack's rendered design-intent entries were read by
that session at their heading lines only (the derivation's §6). So a mark on every unseen sentence would carry no
information. Two marks are carried, and only these:

- **WITHHELD — D-n.** The statement lies inside the home of one of the 22 decisions ruled withheld from L3's pack — the
  member's entry at `the_tabulation_population` → `the_members` → `item_4_identities_inside` names each such identity
  with its home. **An AGREES on such a row is a derived statement reaching a ruled answer the deriving session was not
  shown.**
- **SEEN — §6 item 1.** The statement lies inside the home of one of the design-intent entries whose **heading line
  only** the derivation's §6 stop-on-meeting record says its session met: **D-482 and D-484**, where the session stopped
  reading pack member 05, and **D-335, D-406, D-502 to D-510 and D-504**, which the same heading listing showed near the
  subject. **What was seen was the heading line, and no entry body.** Their homes, located at
  `tools/audit/decisions/backbone_decisions.json` and tested against this population's members: D-482 and D-484 lie
  inside member 36 (`cowork_phrase_boundary_design.md` passages); D-335 inside member 7
  (`cowork_layer5_function_design.md`, whole); D-406 inside member 46 (`cowork_progression_schema_dictionary.md`
  passages); D-502 and D-504 to D-509 inside member 44 (`cowork_progression_schema_design.md` passages); **D-503 and
  D-510 lie inside no member**, and mark no row. Items 2 to 4 of that §6 record name statements of the two input-contract
  files and passages of pack members 02 and 09, none of which is a member of this population. **Where a row names
  L3-S4**, the row says that §6 item 2 of the derivation flags that statement as coinciding with a ruled sentence the
  L0/L1 input contract carries, for the comparison to check.

**The wider check — whether an outgoing statement is the home of any other design-intent entry whose heading the
deriving session's listing of member 05 displayed — is NOT made**, beyond the identities named above, and §14 says so.

## 6. The tabulation, in the published order

Each member's subsection opens with its manifest header and closes with its own arithmetic, and is added in the commit
that tabulates it. Members not yet tabulated have no subsection; §0 names them.

**Two reading rules applied at every member, stated once here so a reader can challenge them at one place.**
**(1) Headings are titles, not sentences**: a section heading is neither an outgoing statement nor an item under *not a
statement*, and where a heading carries a claim, the body sentence that states the same claim is what is tabulated.
**(2) A sentence introduced as the defense of the rule before it** — marked *Why:*, or reasoning offered for that rule —
**is listed under *not a statement* as a defense**, because it states why the analysis does something and not what it
does; the rule it defends is tabulated. Where a defense sentence also states a separate rule of its own, that rule is
tabulated.

### 6.1 — Member 1: `ARCHITECTURE.md`, the section *"#### Layer 4 — the per-slice chord-symbol decoder (Built+Dormant — not wired)"*

> **Manifest for this member.** Position **1**. Kind: *item 1 — a named place*. Document: `ARCHITECTURE.md`. Label:
> *"#### Layer 4 — the per-slice chord-symbol decoder (Built+Dormant — not wired)"*. Range, as a locator only: lines

>
> **What this member is, read whole before any row was written.** The section describes the dormant chord decoder of
> the earlier layer stack, which decides a chord per slice. Under the ratified charter that decision is L2's, and L3
> reads the chord symbol off it as a view; so most of the section's statements are about another charter's subject,
> about how that dormant implementation works, or about its build state, and they are placed accordingly.

**Row 1.18 — the module table: decode a chord symbol per slice.**

*Outgoing statement.* The table row "| `composing/analysis/chord/chordslicedecoder.{h,cpp}` | **Decode a chord symbol per
Layer-2 slice.** `decideSlice` (scorer-independent) selects from a candidate cube, computes the confidence margin to
the best DIFFERENT chord, ranks/caps the alternatives, carries the prevailing (∪) union, and marks "uncertain". `decode`
runs the full pipeline over a Layer-1 note model + `changePointSlices`; `redecodeRange` re-decodes a sub-range under the
same incremental contract. |" — the module table (locator: line 1992).

*Derived statements that speak to it.* L3-S1.

*Current-text axis.* L3-S1: **DIFFERS**.

*The difference, in both texts' own words.* The outgoing text says the module's responsibility is to *"Decode a chord
symbol per Layer-2 slice"*, selecting *"from a candidate cube"*; L3-S1 says the chord symbol is *"the deterministic
function of that span's four chord fields and its tonality"* and that *"Nothing is decided in computing it"*.

*PROPOSED DISPOSITION.* **QUARANTINED.** *Audit question:* do `decideSlice`, `decode` and `redecodeRange` do what the row
says, and is any of them reached at the current commit?

#### Not a statement, at this member

1. "**Plan correction, 2026-08-02 (`OPEN_ITEMS.md` OI-232 item 3; the heading read "engages with L5", which no longer
   describes anything scheduled).**" (1937–1938) — *provenance*.
2. "**Delegation pointer (the fifth home case, user-ratified 2026-08-02).** The ratified contract for this layer is
   `cowork_layer4_chordsymbol_design.md` (SIGNED, user, 2026-06-24) — D-329…D-334 — which this section points at and
   does not restate." (1946) — *a pointer*.
3. "**★ A REBUILT OR RE-TUNED CHORD SCORING MUST NOT RELY ON THE HELD-NOTE REPETITION BONUS THE FAITHFUL NOTE MODEL
   REMOVED (re-homed into this specification 2026-08-08 on the user's ruling).**" (1950–1951) — *a title with
   provenance*, its rule tabulated at Row 1.8.
4. "*Why:* measured when it surfaced — removing the inflation moved a small number of cases the wrong way while the key
   axis stayed flat, …; a correct re-calibration is expected to recover them on real evidence." (1955–1958) — *a
   defense* of Row 1.8.
5. "It is exactly the hidden dependency the upstream-first rebuild exists to surface." (1958–1959) — *a defense*.
6. "**Whether those cases have since recovered is NOT stated here and was not checked** — the constraint binds
   regardless, because it forbids leaning on the artifact rather than asserting anything about the current count."
   (1959–1961) — *a scope remark*.

**Row 3.1 — the grouping layer assembles a flat structure: punctuation-spans, key-areas and the cadence alignment;
additive and read-only.**

*Outgoing statement.* "The grouping layer assembles the L5-labelled stream into the **flat** structure the ground truth
annotates: **punctuation-spans** (from the L1.5 phrase-boundary primitive), **key-areas** (grouping the local-key track,
which **cross-cuts** punctuation-spans — §2.15 span typology), and the **alignment of cadences to punctuation-span ends**
— additive, read-only, no feedback into L5." — the section's opening paragraph (locator: lines 2194–2197). Five claims:
(i) it assembles a flat structure; (ii) the punctuation-spans come from the phrase-boundary primitive of the layer
beneath; (iii) it groups the local-key track into key-areas, which cross-cut the punctuation-spans; (iv) it aligns
cadences to punctuation-span ends; (v) it is additive and read-only, with no feedback.

*Derived statements that speak to it.* (i) L3-S22. (ii) L3-S8. (iii) None. (iv) L3-S10. (v) L3-S38.

*Current-text axis.* (i) L3-S22: **DIFFERS**. (ii) L3-S8: **DIFFERS**. (iii) **THE DERIVATION IS SILENT** — the derivation
publishes no key-area grouping. (iv) L3-S10: **AGREES** — *"The cadence is aligned to the phrase, never the phrase to the
cadence."* (v) L3-S38: **AGREES** — *"No L3 fact may change an L2 fact, a rival or a mass."*

*The difference, in both texts' own words.* (i) The outgoing text assembles *"the **flat** structure the ground truth
annotates"*; L3-S22 says *"It is a nested grouping over the units of the phrase reading … sections at one level tile the
level above"*. (ii) The outgoing text takes the punctuation-spans *"from the L1.5 phrase-boundary primitive"*; L3-S8 says
*"The phrase reading is an L3 decision."*

*PROPOSED DISPOSITION.* (i) **UNPLACED** — read: the two texts above. (ii) **UNPLACED** — read: the two texts above, and
the L2 comparison's record that the L0/L1 comparison relocated the graded phrase-boundary profile to L3. (iii) **ADOPTED
— proposed.** *Proposal:* that L3 publish key areas — runs of the local tonality — as a grouping that may cut across the
phrase units. (iv) **ADOPTED — carried** (L3-S10). (v) **ADOPTED — carried** (L3-S38).

> **How rule (1) was applied at this member, stated so it can be challenged at one place.** The document opens many
> bullets with a bold run-in lead. A lead that is a noun-phrase label (*"Forward-only."*, *"Selection, not
> re-derivation."*, *"The firewall."*) is read as a title under rule (1) and is neither a row nor an item under *not a
> statement*; the body sentence after it is what is tabulated. A lead that is a complete sentence stating a rule

> entries (§12 of the document) are tabulated one row per entry, each placed by its subject.
>
> **What this member is, read whole before any row was written.** The document is the signed design of the earlier
> layer stack's function layer: it derives the Roman numeral from a key and a chord decided by layers beneath it,
> detects cadences key-agnostically and lets them vote for the key, arbitrates tonicization against modulation, resolves
> the chord layer's carried uncertain readings by selection, and fixes a cadence typology and a perfect/imperfect
> criterion. Under the ratified charter most of that is the subject of *L2 — The tonal reading*, which publishes the
> chord as degree, quality, figure and applied target against the tonality, with rivals; the cadence and its type, the
> phrase reading and the chord symbol's read-off are L3's. So most rows are RELOCATED to L2, the cadence-type rules are
> placed against L3-S15 to L3-S20, and the build-state and as-built passages are HISTORICAL or QUARANTINED.

---

**Row 7.1 — the perfect/imperfect call no longer on the top voice; the bass-derived inversion decides.**

*Outgoing statement.* "**Amendment (user-surfaced):** the perfect/imperfect cadence distinction (§5.2) no longer rests
on the **top voice** — the highest sounding voice is not reliably the structural melody (orchestral doubling;
barbershop lead below the top), so the call is made on the **bass-derived inversion** criterion and the top-voice
arrival is only a soft optional nudge." — the status block above §1 (locator: lines 3–6). Two claims: (i) the
perfect/imperfect call is made on the bass-derived inversion criterion; (ii) the top-voice arrival is only a soft
optional nudge.

*Derived statements that speak to it.* (i) L3-S17. (ii) L3-S16.

*Current-text axis.* (i) L3-S17: **DIFFERS**. (ii) L3-S16: **DIFFERS**.

*The difference, in both texts' own words.* (i) The outgoing text makes *"the call … on the **bass-derived inversion**
criterion"*; L3-S17 says *"Feature (6) blocks the PAC/IAC split in every vocabulary until OQ-L3-4 is ruled"*. (ii) The

approach chord moves to the tonic in **that same voice** at the arrival; a **tritone resolution** is detected when the
dominant's tritone (the fourth and seventh degrees) contracts or expands by step to the tonic's third and root across
the boundary." — §5.0, the resolution bullet, after its label (locator: lines 236–239).

*Current-text axis.* **THE DERIVATION IS SILENT**.

resolution and the sounding together of the fourth and seventh degrees are L1's local cadence cues (the derivation's
L3-S21 cites IC-L1 S-46 and S-47 for them).

**Row 8.10 — the glossary entry *Phrase [MT]*: the melodic phrase, not segmented here.**

*Outgoing statement.* "**Phrase [MT] — the accepted term, and NOT what this layer segments.** In music theory a phrase
is a broadly *melodic / linear* unit: essentially **monophonic in conception** (in homophonic or polyphonic textures it
is still carried by a leading line), conventionally closed by a **cadence** (Caplin makes the cadence definitional) or
by a breath/gesture, and — when sung — usually **coinciding with a text phrase**. **This is a construct of the melodic
/ voice-leading dimension, not of harmonic grouping, so L6 does not segment it.** Identifying the accepted phrase — and
the **concurrent, overlapping, out-of-phase per-voice phrases** of a contrapuntal texture (e.g. a fugue's staggered
subject entries) — belongs to the **future voice-leading / melody-line layer** (the confirmed second axis,
`cowork_idiom_discovery_findings.md`); the method foundation to lean on is recorded in
`cowork_polyphony_phrase_harmony_research.md`. What L6 actually groups is a *different* object — the
**punctuation-span**, defined under [L6] below. *(Ref: Caplin 1998 for the cadence-defined phrase; the melodic-grouping
and voice-separation literature for the linear one.)*" — §0 *Terminology*, the first entry (locator: lines 45–55). Three
claims: (i) the accepted phrase is a melodic, linear unit closed by a cadence or a breath; (ii) it is the melodic
dimension's construct, and this layer does not segment it; (iii) identifying it, and the per-voice phrases of a
contrapuntal texture, belongs to the voice-leading layer.

*Derived statements that speak to it.* (i) None. (ii) L3-S42. (iii) L3-S42.

*Current-text axis.* (i) **THE DERIVATION IS SILENT**. (ii) L3-S42: **AGREES** — L3's phrase is *"the harmonic spine's
phrase, which tiles"*, and *"The accepted melodic phrase, which overlaps across voices, is the axis's"*. (iii) L3-S42:
**AGREES** — the same sentence.

*PROPOSED DISPOSITION.* (i) **RELOCATED** — to *the second axis — voice leading*, whose objects include *"the accepted
melodic phrase"* (`FRAMEWORK.md` §5). (ii) **ADOPTED — carried** (L3-S42). (iii) **RELOCATED** — to *the second axis —
voice leading*, as (i).

**Row 8.14 — the glossary entry *Punctuation-span [L6]*: a non-nested unit delimited by the notated punctuation.**

*Outgoing statement.* "**Punctuation-span [L6] — the harmonic-grouping span L6 segments (the DCML `{}` unit).** What
this layer segments is **not** the accepted melodic phrase [MT] above; it is the flat, **non-hierarchical** grouping
span of the DCML / When-in-Rome annotation standard — the `{ }` unit whose boundaries are the score's *notated
punctuation* (fermatas, rests, double barlines, key-signature changes, …), **which is what it is named for**. Its
boundaries are delimited by those **surface cues** via the phrase-boundary primitive (§5.1), **not** by requiring a
cadence (defining a span by its cadence and then aligning cadences to it in §5.3 would be circular). The DCML standard
happens to label this `{}` unit "phrase," but it is a **harmonic / annotation grouping construct** — blind to melody and
voice — and it **diverges** from the accepted melodic phrase [MT]; this design therefore calls it the
**punctuation-span** and never "phrase." **★ Naming convention: the word "phrase" appears in this document only as
"phrase [MT]" (the accepted melodic unit, out of scope); the harmonic-grouping object L6 produces is always the
"punctuation-span."** In chorales the two coincide (a fermata marks both a punctuation-span edge and a cadence); in
general they need not. The cadence↔span relation is *checked* in §5.3, never assumed in the definition. *(Ref: the
DCML annotation standard.)*" — §0, the first operational entry (locator: lines 64–75). Three claims: (i) the grouping
unit is a non-nested span, with no hierarchy; (ii) its boundaries are the notated punctuation, delimited through the
phrase-boundary primitive, and are not fixed by requiring a cadence; (iii) it is a harmonic grouping construct, blind
to melody and voice, distinct from the melodic phrase. The naming convention is about the document's vocabulary.

*Derived statements that speak to it.* (i) L3-S12. (ii) L3-S8 and L3-S10. (iii) L3-S42.

*Current-text axis.* (i) L3-S12: **AGREES** — *"The phrase reading tiles the working span."* (ii) L3-S8: **DIFFERS**;
L3-S10: **AGREES** — *"The cadence is aligned to the phrase, never the phrase to the cadence."* (iii) L3-S42: **AGREES**
— *"L3's phrase is the harmonic spine's phrase, which tiles."*

*The difference, in both texts' own words.* (ii) The outgoing boundaries *"are delimited by those **surface cues** via
the phrase-boundary primitive"*; L3-S8 says *"The phrase reading is an L3 decision"* and *"Every change point and both
span edges are candidate phrase ends."*

*PROPOSED DISPOSITION.* (i) **ADOPTED — carried** (L3-S12). (ii) **UNPLACED** — a rule on L3's own subject that one
derived statement differs from and another agrees with; read: the texts above and OQ-L3-3. (iii) **ADOPTED — carried**
(L3-S42).

**Row 8.23 — the glossary entry *Caplinian formal functions*: out of this layer's scope.**

*Outgoing statement.* "**Caplinian formal functions [MT — out of L6's scope].** William Caplin's theory of classical
form (*Classical Form*, 1998), which defines formal units by their temporal **function** (beginning / middle / end). Its
two core themes: the **sentence** (a *presentation* — a basic idea plus its repetition — then a *continuation*, then a
*cadence*) and the **period** (an *antecedent* phrase [MT] closing on a weaker cadence, e.g. a half cadence, answered by
a *consequent* phrase [MT] closing on a stronger one, e.g. a PAC). These are **hierarchical** constructs *above* the
melodic phrase — a larger structure than L6's flat grouping; §2 and §9-D3 state why they sit outside L6's core and on
what terms they could be built." — §0, the fourth entry of *Other terms* (locator: lines 98–104).

*Derived statements that speak to it.* L3-S24.

*Current-text axis.* L3-S24: **AGREES** — *"L3 publishes the grouping (boundaries, nesting and rivals) and not
form-function names"*.

*PROPOSED DISPOSITION.* **ADOPTED — carried** (L3-S24).

**Member 1.** No ADOPTED — proposed row.

  over the Layer-2 slices"*, where L3-S1 says the chord symbol is *"a view of L2's chord"* and that *"Nothing is decided
  in computing it"*.

  Layer-2 slice"* by selecting *"from a candidate cube"*, where L3-S1 says the chord symbol is *"the deterministic
  function of that span's four chord fields and its tonality"*.

**Member 2.**

- **DIFFERS — Row 2.3(i) (L3-S38):** the outgoing text places *"the cadence-confirmed modulation recompute and the
  fine-grain chord override"* at this layer, where L3-S38 says *"No L3 fact may change an L2 fact, a rival or a mass."*

  principal chord symbol *"always the one read off L2's principal reading"*.

**Member 8.**

- **ADOPTED — proposed — Row 8.12, with Rows 8.17, 8.29, 8.46, 8.63 (ii), 8.74, 8.75 and 8.79 (i):** that L3 publish key
  areas — maximal runs of consecutive spans of L2's segmentation sharing one tonality, each closing at a change of
  tonality — as a grouping of its own.
- **ADOPTED — proposed — Row 8.31 (i), with Rows 8.48 (i), 8.89 and 8.91:** that a grouping unit containing a span whose
  L2 reading is left unresolved carry that mark visibly at that location, unchanged, and be reported fully resolved
  exactly when none of its spans carries one, no confidence threshold entering the test.
- **ADOPTED — proposed — Row 8.37 (i), with Row 8.38 (i):** that L3 never attempt to identify a melody or structural
  line among the voices.
- **ADOPTED — proposed — Row 8.45 (ii):** that each phrase unit carry the notated evidence that opened and closed it,
  with that evidence's scope — the whole texture or one part — so that a local boundary is never presented as a global
  one.
- **ADOPTED — proposed — Row 8.52 (ii), with Rows 8.53, 8.76, 8.82, 8.117 and 8.145:** that a change of tonality may
  fall inside a phrase unit, the key areas cutting across the phrase units and neither nested in the other.
- **ADOPTED — proposed — Row 8.54:** that the grouping have L3 as its only producer, and be the surface a display reads.
- **ADOPTED — proposed — Row 8.105 (i):** that the phrase and section grouping be held as an explanatory output of the
  analysis, not as an accuracy requirement on it.

## 14. The derivation's independence record, relayed

**NOT YET WRITTEN.** Written once, after the last member: the derivation's §6 relayed whole, with no verdict word.
**Stated here because it binds every row:** the wider check — whether an outgoing statement is the home of any
design-intent entry whose heading the deriving session's listing of pack member 05 displayed, other than the
identities §5 names — is NOT made by this comparison.

## 15. What the user is asked to rule in this file

**NOTHING.** The ruling on these dispositions comes separately, framed by the writing side as a decision surface over
these rows. Nothing in this file is a recommendation about that ruling, about the derivation, about the method, or
about any open question.

## 16. What this file does NOT do

- **It applies no disposition.** Every one is a proposal. No outgoing text is edited, and the derivation is untouched.
- **It establishes nothing** (**#19**). Every verdict in it is this session's authored reading, and every one is
  re-placeable by the user at the two quoted texts.
- **It states no verdict on the derivation or on the deriving session's independence.**
- **It adjudicates nothing between a derived statement and the outgoing text.** A DIFFERS row is not a defect finding,
  is not an open-items row, and decides nothing — the phase definition reserves that to the audit.
- **It puts none of the derivation's five ★ questions to anyone.**
